A long-sleeved V-neck cotton T-shirt, a real plain T-shirt and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a bold flat-colour illustration with thick black outlines, designed to make the shirt look like the outfit of a cooper. The print is cut to the T-shirt's shape, so the shirt itself looks like the outfit being worn, with the sleeves as the outfit's sleeves. Every panel of the shirt, front, shoulders and sleeves, is a panel of the outfit, showing a leather apron and rolled sleeves. The print wraps all the way round, the back of the shirt shows the rear of the outfit from the back collar to the hem and along both sleeves. The outfit's own cloth covers the whole shirt as one continuous print, so shoulders, sleeves and body are all in the outfit's colours. The shirt keeps the outline of an ordinary T-shirt, round neckline, straight side seams, sleeves, and a straight open hem. The printed top garment continues down the shirt to its straight hem, with at most a belt or sash at the waist. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the shirt is outfit cloth. The outfit is stretched to the shirt's own straight-sided shape and fills it completely, so it suits any body size. A long-sleeved shirt is used so the outfit's long sleeves fit exactly, printed full length to the shirt's wrist hems. The shirt is solid opaque cotton and every texture and sheen is a printed picture. The print is the garment itself, flat and unfolded over the shirt, in its own cloth and colours. The sleeves are extended to full wrist length so the rolled sleeves that the outfit wears on the forearms can be printed on them, ending exactly at the wrist hems. It also shows printed cuffs where the sleeves end. Tiny printed shadows and fold lines suggest the garment without any real depth. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the most recognisable props and accessories of the outfit as appliqué pieces, the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem and the outfit's badges, insignia and emblems, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.
A long-sleeved V-neck cotton T-shirt, a real plain T-shirt and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a bold flat-colour illustration with thick black outlines, designed to make the shirt look like the outfit of a farmer. No plain shirt fabric shows except the ribbed collar band, because the outfit fills the whole shirt. The shirt's neckline is the outfit's collar opening, the shirt's sleeves carry the outfit's sleeves, and the shirt's hem is the outfit's lower edge, showing a flannel shirt and denim overalls. The back of the shirt is printed just as completely, edge to edge, with the back of the same outfit, its back collar, shoulder blades and the backs of both sleeves. The outfit's fabric runs seamlessly over the shoulders and down the sleeves, every part of the shirt in the outfit's own colours and textures. It is an ordinary T-shirt in shape, with a straight open hem and sleeves cut to match the outfit's sleeves. Only the upper garment is printed, running all the way down to the shirt's straight hem. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the shirt is outfit cloth. The outfit is laid out on the shirt's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. A long-sleeved shirt is used so the outfit's long sleeves fit exactly, printed full length to the shirt's wrist hems. The shirt is solid opaque cotton and every texture and sheen is a printed picture. The picture is only the garment, its cloth, seams, buttons and trims laid flat across the whole shirt like a sewing pattern. It may also show flat printed chest hair on __clothing_uniform_tee_skin__ showing at the open collar, all as flat ink. It also shows printed cuffs at the sleeve hems. The print wraps around the sides so the outfit seems to continue around the body. To read as a costume rather than a plain top, it adds the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, the held item or gear of the outfit, shown as a patch or large motif on the chest or a sleeve and the collar, neckwear and fastenings of the outfit, bold and large enough to identify the outfit from across a room.

The sleeves are extended to full wrist length so the wrist that the outfit wears on the forearms can be printed on them, ending exactly at the wrist hems. It is a trompe-l'oeil T-shirt, a plain cotton shirt pretending to be the outfit. To read as a costume rather than a plain top, it adds the collar, neckwear and fastenings of the outfit, the most recognisable props and accessories of the outfit as appliqué pieces and the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, bold and large enough to identify the outfit from across a room.

A long-sleeved crew-neck cotton T-shirt, a real plain T-shirt and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a comic-style inked illustration print, designed to make the shirt look like the outfit of a welder. The print covers every part of the shirt from the ribbed collar band to the hem and out to both sleeve hems, and wraps around the side seams to the back. Every panel of the shirt, front, shoulders and sleeves, is a panel of the outfit, showing a leather welding jacket and gauntlets. Front and back are both fully printed, the back showing how the same outfit looks from behind. The outfit's fabric runs seamlessly over the shoulders and down the sleeves, every part of the shirt in the outfit's own colours and textures. It is an ordinary T-shirt in shape, with a straight open hem and sleeves cut to match the outfit's sleeves. Only the upper garment is printed, running all the way down to the shirt's straight hem. The print is cut exactly to the shirt, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The outfit is laid out on the shirt's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The shirt is a long-sleeved T-shirt matching the outfit's long sleeves, printed with the sleeves and cuffs all the way down to the wrist hems. The shirt's sleeves are tailored to the outfit's cut, wide or flared where the outfit's sleeves flare, ending at the same slant or curve, with the print filling the whole sleeve shape. All textures, transparencies and sheens are printed patterns on solid cotton. The picture is only the garment, its cloth, seams, buttons and trims laid flat across the whole shirt like a sewing pattern. The outfit has gauntlets on the forearm and wrist, so the shirt's sleeves are lengthened to the wrist and the printed gauntlets sits at the end of each sleeve, with nothing beyond the wrist hem. It also shows a printed collar around the real neckline and printed gloves emerging at the sleeve hems. The illusion is purely flat and two-dimensional, with no real fabric, buttons or pockets beyond the print. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the collar, neckwear and fastenings of the outfit, the outfit's badges, insignia and emblems and the held item or gear of the outfit, shown as a patch or large motif on the chest or a sleeve, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.

The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the shirt is outfit cloth. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided shirt shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The shirt is a long-sleeved T-shirt matching the outfit's long sleeves, printed with the sleeves and cuffs all the way down to the wrist hems. All textures, transparencies and sheens are printed patterns on solid cotton. The picture is only the garment, its cloth, seams, buttons and trims laid flat across the whole shirt like a sewing pattern. It also shows printed cuffs where the sleeves end. It is a flat print, not a framed picture, not a small centred graphic, not a standing figure and not a drawing of a garment on a hanger. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the headwear's emblem or crest worked into the chest, the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem and the most recognisable props and accessories of the outfit as appliqué pieces, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.

The outfit is printed across the whole front, over the shoulders and down both sleeves, showing a work shirt with a pocket of testers. The back of the shirt is printed just as completely, edge to edge, with the back of the same outfit, its back collar, shoulder blades and the backs of both sleeves. The outfit's fabric runs seamlessly over the shoulders and down the sleeves, every part of the shirt in the outfit's own colours and textures. The shirt keeps the outline of an ordinary T-shirt, round neckline, straight side seams, sleeves, and a straight open hem. Only the upper garment is printed, running all the way down to the shirt's straight hem. The print is cut exactly to the shirt, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided shirt shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The shirt is a long-sleeved T-shirt matching the outfit's long sleeves, printed with the sleeves and cuffs all the way down to the wrist hems. Everything is flat ink on opaque cotton, lace, tulle, fur, feathers, sequins, armour and metal appear as printed patterns. The print is the garment itself, flat and unfolded over the shirt, in its own cloth and colours. It may also show flat printed skin in __clothing_uniform_tee_skin__ at the neckline, with fine printed body hair where it would naturally show, all as flat ink. It also shows a printed collar continuing the outfit up to the neckline and printed gloves emerging at the sleeve hems. It is a trompe-l'oeil T-shirt, a plain cotton shirt pretending to be the outfit. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the collar, neckwear and fastenings of the outfit, the outfit's badges, insignia and emblems and the held item or gear of the outfit, shown as a patch or large motif on the chest or a sleeve, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.
A long-sleeved straight-cut cotton T-shirt, a real plain T-shirt and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a hyper-realistic sublimation print showing the real outfit in fine detail, designed to make the shirt look like the outfit of a cooper. The print is cut to the T-shirt's shape, so the shirt itself looks like the outfit being worn, with the sleeves as the outfit's sleeves. The shirt's neckline is the outfit's collar opening, the shirt's sleeves carry the outfit's sleeves, and the shirt's hem is the outfit's lower edge, showing a leather apron and rolled sleeves. Turn the shirt around and the back carries the matching back of the outfit, printed edge to edge with nothing left plain. The outfit's own cloth covers the whole shirt as one continuous print, so shoulders, sleeves and body are all in the outfit's colours. The shirt is a standard T-shirt shape with a straight hem across the bottom. Only the upper garment is printed, running all the way down to the shirt's straight hem. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the shirt is outfit cloth. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided shirt shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. A long-sleeved shirt is used so the outfit's long sleeves fit exactly, printed full length to the shirt's wrist hems. All textures, transparencies and sheens are printed patterns on solid cotton. The print is the garment itself, flat and unfolded over the shirt, in its own cloth and colours. It may also show a flat printed bare chest in __clothing_uniform_tee_skin__ with printed chest hair where the outfit is worn open, all as flat ink. The sleeves are extended to full wrist length so the rolled sleeves that the outfit wears on the forearms can be printed on them, ending exactly at the wrist hems. The print wraps around the sides so the outfit seems to continue around the body. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem, the held item or gear of the outfit, shown as a patch or large motif on the chest or a sleeve and the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.
A long-sleeved straight-cut cotton T-shirt, a real plain T-shirt and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a hyper-realistic sublimation print showing the real outfit in fine detail, designed to make the shirt look like the outfit of a newspaper vendor. The outfit's colours flood the entire front and both sleeves as one continuous print, and the back carries the matching back of the outfit. Every panel of the shirt, front, shoulders and sleeves, is a panel of the outfit, showing a flat cap, a waistcoat and rolled sleeves. The print wraps all the way round, the back of the shirt shows the rear of the outfit from the back collar to the hem and along both sleeves. The outfit's fabric runs seamlessly over the shoulders and down the sleeves, every part of the shirt in the outfit's own colours and textures. The shirt is a standard T-shirt shape with a straight hem across the bottom. The printed top garment continues down the shirt to its straight hem, with at most a belt or sash at the waist. The outfit's collar meets the real collar band, its shoulders sit on the real shoulder seams and its sleeves fill the real sleeves, all in one piece of printed cloth. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided shirt shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The shirt is a long-sleeved T-shirt matching the outfit's long sleeves, printed with the sleeves and cuffs all the way down to the wrist hems. Everything is flat ink on opaque cotton, lace, tulle, fur, feathers, sequins, armour and metal appear as printed patterns. The print is the garment itself, flat and unfolded over the shirt, in its own cloth and colours. The outfit has rolled sleeves on the forearm and wrist, so the shirt's sleeves are lengthened to the wrist and the printed rolled sleeves sits at the end of each sleeve, with nothing beyond the wrist hem. It also shows printed cuffs where the sleeves end. It is the kind of novelty tee that tricks the eye from across a room. It carries enough of the costume to be unmistakable, the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, the most recognisable props and accessories of the outfit as appliqué pieces and the outfit's badges, insignia and emblems, all in the outfit's own colours.

Every panel of the shirt, front, shoulders and sleeves, is a panel of the outfit, showing a leather apron over a linen shirt. The back of the shirt is printed just as completely, edge to edge, with the back of the same outfit, its back collar, shoulder blades and the backs of both sleeves. One continuous print of the outfit's cloth covers the shirt from collar band to hem and out to both sleeve hems. It is an ordinary T-shirt in shape, with a straight open hem and sleeves cut to match the outfit's sleeves. The printed top garment continues down the shirt to its straight hem, with at most a belt or sash at the waist. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the shirt is outfit cloth. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided shirt shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. A short-sleeved shirt is used, and the outfit is printed as short sleeves that end at the shirt's sleeve hems. Everything is flat ink on opaque cotton, lace, tulle, fur, feathers, sequins, armour and metal appear as printed patterns. Only the outfit's fabric, trims and fastenings are pictured, spread across the entire shirt as a continuous pattern. It may also show a flat printed line of cleavage in __clothing_uniform_tee_skin__ at a low or sweetheart neckline, all as flat ink. It also shows a printed collar around the real neckline and printed cuffs where the sleeves end. It is a flat print, not a framed picture, not a small centred graphic, not a standing figure and not a drawing of a garment on a hanger. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit, the outfit's badges, insignia and emblems and the collar, neckwear and fastenings of the outfit, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.
A short-sleeved heavyweight cotton T-shirt, a real plain T-shirt and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a pop-art halftone print, designed to make the shirt look like the outfit of a blacksmith. The print covers every part of the shirt from the ribbed collar band to the hem and out to both sleeve hems, and wraps around the side seams to the back. The shirt's neckline is the outfit's collar opening, the shirt's sleeves carry the outfit's sleeves, and the shirt's hem is the outfit's lower edge, showing a leather apron over a sooty linen shirt. The print wraps all the way round, the back of the shirt shows the rear of the outfit from the back collar to the hem and along both sleeves. One continuous print of the outfit's cloth covers the shirt from collar band to hem and out to both sleeve hems. The shirt keeps the outline of an ordinary T-shirt, round neckline, straight side seams, sleeves, and a straight open hem. The shirt is an ordinary T-shirt ending in a straight bottom hem, and the garment printed on it runs straight down the whole shirt to that hem. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the shirt is outfit cloth. The outfit is laid out on the shirt's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. A short-sleeved shirt is used, and the outfit is printed as short sleeves that end at the shirt's sleeve hems. All textures, transparencies and sheens are printed patterns on solid cotton. Only the outfit's fabric, trims and fastenings are pictured, spread across the entire shirt as a continuous pattern. It may also show flat printed skin in __clothing_uniform_tee_skin__ at the neckline, with fine printed body hair where it would naturally show, all as flat ink. It also shows printed cuffs at the sleeve hems. It is the kind of novelty tee that tricks the eye from across a room. To read as a costume rather than a plain top, it adds the headwear's emblem or crest worked into the chest, the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem and the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem, bold and large enough to identify the outfit from across a room.

The shirt's neckline is the outfit's collar opening, the shirt's sleeves carry the outfit's sleeves, and the shirt's hem is the outfit's lower edge, showing a clay-stained apron over a loose shirt. The back of the shirt is printed just as completely, edge to edge, with the back of the same outfit, its back collar, shoulder blades and the backs of both sleeves. The outfit's fabric runs seamlessly over the shoulders and down the sleeves, every part of the shirt in the outfit's own colours and textures. The shirt keeps the outline of an ordinary T-shirt, round neckline, straight side seams, sleeves, and a straight open hem. The printed top garment continues down the shirt to its straight hem, with at most a belt or sash at the waist. The print is cut exactly to the shirt, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The outfit is laid out on the shirt's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The shirt is a short-sleeved T-shirt, and the outfit's sleeves are printed as short sleeves ending exactly at the shirt's sleeve hems. All textures, transparencies and sheens are printed patterns on solid cotton. The picture is only the garment, its cloth, seams, buttons and trims laid flat across the whole shirt like a sewing pattern. It may also show flat printed belly hair on __clothing_uniform_tee_skin__ showing at a bare midriff or between shirt buttons, all as flat ink. It also shows printed cuffs and wrists at the sleeve hems. Tiny printed shadows and fold lines suggest the garment without any real depth. It carries enough of the costume to be unmistakable, the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, the collar, neckwear and fastenings of the outfit and the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem, all in the outfit's own colours.
A short-sleeved ringer-style cotton T-shirt, a real plain T-shirt and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a retro screen-printed illustration with limited colours, designed to make the shirt look like the outfit of a potter. The outfit's colours flood the entire front and both sleeves as one continuous print, and the back carries the matching back of the outfit. Every panel of the shirt, front, shoulders and sleeves, is a panel of the outfit, showing a clay-stained apron over a loose shirt. Front and back are both fully printed, the back showing how the same outfit looks from behind. One continuous print of the outfit's cloth covers the shirt from collar band to hem and out to both sleeve hems. The shirt keeps the outline of an ordinary T-shirt, round neckline, straight side seams, sleeves, and a straight open hem. The shirt is an ordinary T-shirt ending in a straight bottom hem, and the garment printed on it runs straight down the whole shirt to that hem. The print is cut exactly to the shirt, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided shirt shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The shirt is a short-sleeved T-shirt, and the outfit's sleeves are printed as short sleeves ending exactly at the shirt's sleeve hems. All textures, transparencies and sheens are printed patterns on solid cotton. The print is the garment itself, flat and unfolded over the shirt, in its own cloth and colours. It may also show flat printed chest hair on __clothing_uniform_tee_skin__ showing at the open collar, all as flat ink. The illusion is purely flat and two-dimensional, with no real fabric, buttons or pockets beyond the print. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, its pockets, flaps and trims and the outfit's badges, insignia and emblems, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.

It also shows printed cuffs and wrists at the sleeve hems. It is a trompe-l'oeil T-shirt, a plain cotton shirt pretending to be the outfit. It also shows a printed collar around the real neckline and printed cuffs where the sleeves end. It is a trompe-l'oeil T-shirt, a plain cotton shirt pretending to be the outfit. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit, the most recognisable props and accessories of the outfit as appliqué pieces and the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.

Every panel of the shirt, front, shoulders and sleeves, is a panel of the outfit, showing a high-visibility vest over a work shirt. Front and back are both fully printed, the back showing how the same outfit looks from behind. One continuous print of the outfit's cloth covers the shirt from collar band to hem and out to both sleeve hems. The shirt keeps the outline of an ordinary T-shirt, round neckline, straight side seams, sleeves, and a straight open hem. The shirt is an ordinary T-shirt ending in a straight bottom hem, and the garment printed on it runs straight down the whole shirt to that hem. The print is cut exactly to the shirt, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The outfit is laid out on the shirt's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The shirt is cut sleeveless, with armholes where the outfit's sleeves would start. Everything is flat ink on opaque cotton, lace, tulle, fur, feathers, sequins, armour and metal appear as printed patterns. Only the outfit's fabric, trims and fastenings are pictured, spread across the entire shirt as a continuous pattern. It may also show flat printed skin in __clothing_uniform_tee_skin__ at the neckline, with fine printed body hair where it would naturally show, all as flat ink. It is a flat print, not a framed picture, not a small centred graphic, not a standing figure and not a drawing of a garment on a hanger. To read as a costume rather than a plain top, it adds the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem, the collar, neckwear and fastenings of the outfit and the headwear's emblem or crest worked into the chest, bold and large enough to identify the outfit from across a room.
